in particular the operators
T_{F2}
that maps
\mathcal{SH}(U)
into
\mathcal{AM}(U)
is the Laplace operator in dimension four.

It is possible to factorize the Laplace operator in the following two different ways. Precisely,
if
D
and
\overline{{D}}

are the Cauchy-Fueter operator and its conjugate, respectively that are a particular case of (
1.1
) for
n=3
, then
we have

\Delta={D}\overline{{D}}=\overline{{D}}{D}.

Even though the operators
{D}
and
\overline{{D}}
commute
the factorizations
{D}\overline{{D}}
and
\overline{{D}}{D}

give rise to different functions spaces according to the order

{D}
and
\overline{{D}}
applied to a slice hyperholomorphic function.

In modern terminology, the Fueter or Fueter-Sce extension theorem primarily refers to its second map denoted by
T_{F2}
for the quaternions and by
T_{FS2}
for the Clifford algebra setting.
Specifically, this involves the application of the four dimensional Laplacian
\Delta
in the quaternionic case, and the
(n+1)
-dimensional Laplacian
\Delta_{n+1}
raised to the power of
h_{n}:=(n-1)/2
in the Clifford algebra context, where
n
is the number of imaginary units of the Clifford algebra
\mathbb{R}_{n}
and the number
h_{n}
is called Sce exponent.

The notion of fine structures on the
S
-spectrum emerged from observing that various factorizations of the second map
T_{F2}
in the Fueter-Sce extension theorem generated different function spaces with integral representations, allowing the definition of functional calculi for quaternionic operators or paravector operators in the Clifford algebra setting. This observation is formalized in the following definition of fine structures.

Consequently, in our discussion, we will focus only on this second mapping
and omit the original extension theorem that begins with holomorphic functions,
as it is not directly relevant to our purposes.
So, if we first apply operator
{D}
to functions in
\mathcal{SH}(U)
we have:

\begin{CD}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{SH}(U)}@>{D}>{}>{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{AH}(U)}@>{\ \ {\overline{D}}}>{}>{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{AM}(U)},\end{CD}

where
\mathcal{AH}(U)=\{Df\,:\,f\in\mathcal{SH}(U)\}
and
\mathcal{AM}(U)=\{\Delta f\,:\,f\in\mathcal{SH}(U)\}
are the class of axially harmonic function and of axially monogenic functions, respectively.
In the case we apply
\overline{{D}}
to functions in
\mathcal{SH}(U)
we obtain

\begin{CD}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{SH}(U)}@>{\overline{D}}>{}>{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{AP}_{2}(U)}@>{\ \ {D}}>{}>{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{AM}(U)},\end{CD}

where
\mathcal{AP}_{2}(U)=\{\overline{D}f\,:\,f\in\mathcal{SH}(U)\}
is the class of axially polyanalytic functions of order 2.

The integral representations of functions within the fine structures give rise to various functional calculi. The original idea stems from the complex Riesz-Dunford functional calculus
[
63
]
, which is based on the Cauchy integral formula. This calculus allows a complex variable
z
in a suitable holomorphic function
f(z)
to be replaced with a bounded linear operator
A

thereby defining
f(A)
. In the case of fine structures, the integral formulas of the function spaces provide the fundamental tool to define functional calculus, following the same spirit as the Riesz-Dunford calculus.

Remark 10.2

(Developments of the spectral theory on the
S
-spectrum)
.

Recently, it has been shown that both quaternionic and Clifford frameworks are specific cases within a more general context where spectral theory based on the
S
-spectrum can be developed, as detailed in
[
42
,
44
]
and related references.
Using the concept of the
S
-spectrum, researchers have also successfully established a quaternionic version of the spectral theorem. For instance, the spectral theorem for unitary operators, leveraging Herglotz functions, is demonstrated in
[
6
]
, while the quaternionic spectral theorem for normal operators is proved in
[
5
]
. Furthermore, the spectral theorem grounded in the
S
-spectrum has recently been extended to Clifford operators, as discussed in
[
43
]
.

The development of the spectral theory on the
S
-spectrum also has opened up several research directions in hypercomplex analysis and operator theory. Without claiming completeness, we mention the slice hyperholomorphic Schur analysis
[
9
]
, the characteristic operator functions
[
10
]
, the quaternionic perturbation theory and invariant subspaces
[
26
]
, and new classes of fractional diffusion problems that are based on the
H^{\infty}
-version of the
S
-functional calculus
[
35
,
36
,
40
,
41
,
45
]
. Moreover, recently nuclear operators and Grothendieck-Lidskii formula for quaternionic operators has been studied in
[
28
]
and quaternionic triangular linear operators have been investigated in
[
27
]
.

Remark 10.3

(The functional calculi for unbounded operators)
.

The generalization of the holomorphic functional calculus to sectorial operators leads
to the
H^{\infty}
-functional calculus that, in the complex setting, was introduced in the paper
[
78
]
, see also the books
[
68
,
69
,
70
]
. Moreover, the boundedness of the
H^{\infty}
functional calculus depends on suitable quadratic estimates and this calculus has several applications to boundary value problems, see
[
13
,
14
,
15
,
64
]
.
We remark that the
H^{\infty}
-functional calculus exists also for the monogenic functional calculus (see
[
72
]
) and it was introduced by A. McIntosh and his collaborators, see the books
[
71
,
84
]
for more details.

In the quaternionic setting the functional calculi for bounded operators and slice hyperholomorphic functions
have already done in
[
47
,
52
]
, for bounded operators and axially monogenic functions in
[
29
,
31
,
38
,
48
,
49
,
54
]
and for polyanalytic functions and axially monogenic functions more recently in
[
32
,
33
,
59
,
60
]
.

Unbounded operators for a restricted class of functions were considered in
[
30
,
49
,
52
,
66
]
. In the literature there exists the
H^{\infty}
-functional calculus for slice hyperholomorphic functions in
[
7
,
37
]
and for harmonic functions in
[
61
]
.
In the
[
46
]
we enlarges the class of admissible operators
T
for the
S
- and the
Q
-functional calculus to operators of type
(\alpha,\beta,\omega)
. On the other hand we also treat the two
H^{\infty}
-functional calculus for polynomially growing functions which are polyanalytic of order
2
and for axially monogenic functions.

Remark 10.4

(Summary)
.

In this paper we have proved that although the factorizations of the Fueter-Sce map
\Delta_{n+1}^{h}
, with
h_{n}:=(n-1)/2
, may seem intricate at first glance, they can be succinctly summarized in the following table, where we recall

T_{FS}^{(I)}:=\Delta_{n+1}^{\ell-1}D,\quad\quad T_{FS}^{(II)}=D^{s}\overline{D}^{h_{n}-\ell-1},\quad\quad T^{(III)}_{FS}=\Delta_{n+1}^{\ell}\overline{D}^{h_{n}-\ell}

Operators

Regularities

T_{FS}^{(I)},\hskip 8.50012pt1\leq\ell\leq h_{n}

Polyharmonic of degree
h_{n}-\ell+1

T_{FS}^{(II)},\hskip 17.00024pt0\leq\ell\leq h_{n}-2,\hskip 8.50012pt0\leq s\leq h_{n}-1
,
s+\ell<h_{n}

Holomorphic Cliffordian of order
(\ell+1,h_{n}-s-\ell)
,

T^{(III)}_{FS},\hskip 8.50012pt0\leq\ell\leq h_{n}-1

Polyanalytic of order
h_{n}-\ell+1
.

The above table of operators is coherent with the constructions developed in the papers
[
32
,
33
,
59
,
60
]
.

11.
Appendix

Here we give the proof of a crucial technical results.
We start with a simple observation collected in the following lemma.

Lemma 11.1
.

Let
s
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
then we have

\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}(x)+(s-\overline{x})s-\overline{x}(s-\overline{x})=2(s-\overline{x})(s-x_{0}).

Proof.

It follows from the definition of
\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}(x)
.
∎

Lemma 11.2
.

Let
n
be a fixed odd number and
h_{n}:=(n-1)/2
. We assume that
s
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
such that
s\notin[x]
, and
\alpha\in\mathbb{N}
. Then we have

\overline{D}\left[(s-\overline{x})\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-\alpha}(x)\right]=2(h_{n}-\alpha)\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-\alpha}(x)+4\alpha(s-\overline{x})(s-x_{0})\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-\alpha-1}(x),

Proof.

By using the fact that
\partial_{x_{0}}\mathcal{Q}^{-\alpha}_{c,s}(x)=\alpha(2s-2x_{0})\mathcal{Q}^{-\alpha-1}_{c,s}(x)
and
\partial_{x_{i}}(\mathcal{Q}^{-\alpha}_{c,s}(x))=-\alpha(2x_{i})\mathcal{Q}^{-\alpha-1}_{c,s}(x)
, we have

\begin{split}&\overline{D}\left[(s-\overline{x})\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-\alpha}(x)\right]=2h_{n}\mathcal{Q}^{-\alpha}_{c,s}(x)+2\alpha(s-\bar{x})(s-x_{0})\mathcal{Q}^{-\alpha-1}_{c,s}(x)+2\alpha\underline{x}(s-\bar{x})\mathcal{Q}^{-\alpha-1}_{c,s}(x)\\
&=2h_{n}\mathcal{Q}^{-\alpha}_{c,s}(x)+2\alpha[(s-\bar{x})s-\bar{x}(s-\bar{x})]\mathcal{Q}^{-\alpha-1}_{c,s}(x)\\
&=2(h_{n}-\alpha)\mathcal{Q}^{-\alpha}_{c,s}(x)+2\alpha[\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}(x)+(s-\bar{x})s-\bar{x}(s-\bar{x})]\mathcal{Q}^{-\alpha-1}_{c,s}(x)\\
\end{split}

By Lemma
11.1
, we get the result in the following.
∎

We prove Proposition
8.7
stated in Section 8.

Proposition 11.3
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
n
be a fixed odd number and
h_{n}:=(n-1)/2
. We assume that
s
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
such that
s\notin[x]
. If
\beta=2m+1
we have

\displaystyle\overline{D}^{\beta}S^{-1}_{L}(s,x)=\frac{2^{2m+1}}{(h_{n}-2m-2)!}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{m}2^{2j}(m+j)!(h_{n}-m-j-1)!\binom{m+j}{2j}(s-x_{0})^{2j}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-1}(x)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+(s-\overline{x})\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}2^{2j+1}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!\binom{m+j+1}{2j+1}(s-x_{0})^{2j+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)\right)

(11.1)

\displaystyle+4^{2m+1}(2m+1)!(s-\overline{x})(s-x_{0})^{2m+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-2}(x).

If
\beta=2m
we have

(11.2)

\displaystyle\overline{D}^{\beta}S^{-1}_{L}(s,x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2^{2m}}{(h_{n}-2m-1)!}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}2^{2j+1}(m+j)!(h_{n}-m-j-1)!\binom{m+j}{2j+1}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times(s-x_{0})^{2j+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-1}(x)

\displaystyle\left.+(s-\overline{x})\sum_{j=0}^{m-2}2^{2j}(m+j)!(h_{n}-m-j-1)!\binom{m+j}{2j}(s-x_{0})^{2j}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-1}(x)\right)

\displaystyle+4^{2m}(2m)!(s-\overline{x})(s-x_{0})^{2m}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-1}(x),

Proof.

We prove the result by induction over
m\in\mathbb{N}
. We prove first
m=0
. By Lemma
11.2
, we have

\overline{D}S^{-1}_{L}(s,x)=\overline{D}\left((s-\bar{x})\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}(x)^{-1}\right)=2(h_{n}-1)\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-1}(x)+4(s-\overline{x})(s-x_{0})\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2}(x),

which is formula (
11.1
) when
m=0
if we understood the sum
\sum_{k=0}^{-1}
to be zero. We have to prove the inductive step. Let us suppose formula (
11.1
) holds to be true for
m\in\mathbb{N}
. We have to prove the formula (
11.1
) for
m
replaced by
m+1
. By the inductive hypothesis Lemma
11.2
, and Proposition
4.13
we get:

\begin{split}&\overline{D}^{\beta+1}\left(S^{-1}_{L}(s,x)\right)=\frac{2^{2m+1}}{(h_{n}-2m-2)!}\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}2^{2j}(m+j)!(h_{n}-m-j-1)!\binom{m+j}{2j}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\overline{D}\left((s-x_{0})^{2j}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-1}(x)\right)\\
&+\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}2^{2j+1}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!\binom{m+j+1}{2j+1}\overline{D}\left((s-\overline{x})(s-x_{0})^{2j+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)\right)\Bigg)\\
&+4^{2m+1}(2m+1)!\overline{D}\Bigg((s-\overline{x})(s-x_{0})^{2m+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-2}(x)\Bigg)\end{split}

and by Proposition
4.13
and Lemma
11.2
we get

(11.3)

\displaystyle\overline{D}^{\beta+1}\left(S^{-1}_{L}(s,x)\right)=\frac{2^{2m+1}}{(h_{n}-2m-2)!}\Bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}2^{2j}(m+j)!(-2j)(h_{n}-m-j-1)!\binom{m+j}{2j}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times(s-x_{0})^{2j-1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-1}(x)

\displaystyle+(s-\bar{x})\sum_{j=0}^{m}2^{2j+1}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-1)!\binom{m+j}{2j}(s-x_{0})^{2j}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)

\displaystyle+(s-\overline{x})\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}2^{2j+1}(-2j-1)(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!\binom{m+j+1}{2j+1}(s-x_{0})^{2j}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}2^{2j+2}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)\binom{m+j+1}{2j+1}(s-x_{0})^{2j+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)

\displaystyle+(s-\bar{x})\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}2^{2j+3}(m+j+2)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!\binom{m+j+1}{2j+1}(s-x_{0})^{2j+2}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-3}(x)\Bigg)

\displaystyle+4^{2m+1}(-2m-1)(2m+1)!(s-\overline{x})(s-x_{0})^{2m}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-2}(x)

\displaystyle+4^{2m+1}2(h_{n}-2m-2)(2m+1)!(s-x_{0})^{2m+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-2}(x)

\displaystyle+4^{2m+2}(2m+2)!(s-\bar{x})(s-x_{0})^{2m+2}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-3}(x).

Now we split the computations in two parts. In the first part we make the summation of the terms without the factor
(s-\bar{x})
, and in the second part we make the summation of the terms with
(s-\bar{x})
.

First step A
: summation of all the terms without
(s-\bar{x})
. The terms we want to sum are

\begin{split}&\frac{2^{2m+1}}{(h-2m-2)!}\Bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}2^{2j}(m+j)!(-2j)(h_{n}-m-j-1)!\binom{m+j}{2j}(s-x_{0})^{2j-1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-1}(x)\\
&+\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}2^{2j+2}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)\binom{m+j+1}{2j+1}(s-x_{0})^{2j+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)\\
&+2^{2m+2}(h_{n}-2m-2)(h_{n}-2m-2)!(2m+1)!(s-x_{0})^{2m+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-2}(x)\Bigg).\end{split}

In the second summation we make a change of index:
j^{\prime}=j+1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{2^{2m+1}}{(h-2m-2)!}\Bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}2^{2j}(m+j)!(-2j)(h_{n}-m-j-1)!\binom{m+j}{2j}(s-x_{0})^{2j-1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-1}(x)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{m}2^{2j^{\prime}}(m+j^{\prime})!(h_{n}-m-j^{\prime}-1)!(h_{n}-m-j^{\prime}-1)\binom{m+j^{\prime}}{2j^{\prime}-1}(s-x_{0})^{2j^{\prime}-1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j^{\prime}-1}(x)

\displaystyle+2^{2m+2}(h_{n}-2m-2)(h_{n}-2m-2)!(2m+1)!(s-x_{0})^{2m+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-2}(x)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{2m+1}}{(h_{n}-2m-2)!}\Bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m}2^{2j}(m+j)!(h_{n}-m-j-1)!\Bigg[(-2j)\binom{m+j}{2j}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+(h_{n}-m-j-1)\binom{m+j}{2j-1}\Bigg](s-x_{0})^{2j-1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-1}(x)

\displaystyle+2^{2m+2}(h_{n}-2m-2)(h_{n}-2m-2)!(2m+1)!(s-x_{0})^{2m+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-2}(x)\Bigg).

Since
(-2j)\binom{m+j}{2j}+(h_{n}-m-j-1)\binom{m+j}{2j-1}=\binom{m+j}{2j-1}(h_{n}-2m-2)
, we have

(11.4)

\begin{split}&A_{1}=\frac{2^{2m+1}}{(h_{n}-2m-3)!}\Bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m+1}2^{2j}(m+j)!(h_{n}-m-j-1)!\binom{m+j}{2j-1}(s-x_{0})^{2j-1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-1}(x)\Bigg).\end{split}

Second step A
. Now we make the summation of the terms in (
11.3
) with the factor
(s-\bar{x})
. We note that in this summation we do not consider the last term in (
11.3
) since it appears in the correct form without any needs to be manipulated. The terms we want to sum are

\begin{split}&\frac{2^{2m+1}(s-\bar{x})}{(h-2m-2)!}\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}2^{2j+1}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-1)!\binom{m+j}{2j}(s-x_{0})^{2j}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)\\
&+\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}2^{2j+1}(-2j-1)(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!\binom{m+j+1}{2j+1}(s-x_{0})^{2j}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)\\
&+\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}2^{2j+3}(m+j+2)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!\binom{m+j+1}{2j+1}(s-x_{0})^{2j+2}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-3}(x)\\
&+2^{2m+1}(h_{n}-2m-2)!(-2m-1)(2m+1)!(s-x_{0})^{2m}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-2}(x)\Bigg).\end{split}

Now we make a change of index in the third series:
j^{\prime}=j+1
, and we get

\displaystyle\frac{2^{2m+1}(s-\bar{x})}{(h_{n}-2m-2)!}\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}2^{2j+1}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-1)!\binom{m+j}{2j}(s-x_{0})^{2j}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}2^{2j+1}(-2j-1)(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!\binom{m+j+1}{2j+1}(s-x_{0})^{2j}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j^{\prime}=1}^{m}2^{2j^{\prime}+1}(m+j^{\prime}+1)!(h_{n}-m-j^{\prime}-1)!\binom{m+j^{\prime}}{2j^{\prime}-1}(s-x_{0})^{2j^{\prime}}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j^{\prime}-2}(x)

(A2)

\displaystyle+2^{2m+1}(h_{n}-2m-2)!(-2m-1)(2m+1)!(s-x_{0})^{2m}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-2}(x)\Bigg).

Now we make the summation of the terms
j=0
in formula
(A2)
:

(11.5)

\begin{split}&\frac{2^{2m+1}(s-\bar{x})}{(h_{n}-2m-2)!}\Bigg(2(m+1)!(h_{n}-m-1)!\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-2}(x)-2(m+1)!(h_{n}-m-2)!(m+1)\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-2}(x)\Bigg)\\
&=\frac{2^{2m+1}(s-\bar{x})}{(h_{n}-2m-3)!}\Bigg(2(h_{n}-m-2)!(m+1)!\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-2}(x)\Bigg).\end{split}

Now we make the summation of the terms
j=m
plus the last term in formula
(A_{2})
:

(11.6)

\displaystyle\frac{2^{2m+1}(s-\bar{x})}{(h_{n}-2m-2)!}\Bigg(2^{2m+1}(2m+1)!(h_{n}-2m-1)!(s-x_{0})^{2m}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-2}(x)

\displaystyle+2^{2m+1}(2m+1)!(h_{n}-2m-1)!2m(s-x_{0})^{2m}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-2}(x)

\displaystyle+2^{2m+1}(h_{n}-2m-2)!(-2m-1)(2m+1)!(s-x_{0})^{2m}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-2}(x)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{2m+1}(s-\bar{x})}{(h_{n}-2m-2)!}\Bigg(2^{2m+1}(2m+1)!(h_{n}-2m-2)!(s-\bar{x})(s-x_{0})^{2m}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-2}(x)

\displaystyle\times\left(h_{n}-2m-1+2m(h_{n}-2m-1)-2m-1\right)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{2m+1}(s-\bar{x})}{(h_{n}-2m-3)!}\Bigg(2^{2m+1}(2m+1)(2m+1)!(h_{n}-2m-2)!(s-x_{0})^{2m}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-2}(x)\Bigg).

Now we make the summation of the terms in formula
(A2)
with
j=1,\dots,m-1
:

(11.7)

\begin{split}&\frac{2^{2m+1}(s-\bar{x})}{(h-2m-2)!}\Bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m-1}2^{2j+1}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-1)!\binom{m+j}{2j}(s-x_{0})^{2j}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)\\
&+\sum_{j=1}^{m-1}2^{2j+1}(-2j-1)(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!\binom{m+j+1}{2j+1}(s-x_{0})^{2j}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)\\
&+\sum_{j=1}^{m-1}2^{2j+1}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-1)!\binom{m+j}{2j-1}(s-x_{0})^{2j}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)\Bigg)\\
&=\frac{2^{2m+1}(s-\bar{x})}{(h-2m-2)!}\Bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m-1}2^{2j+1}(m+j+1)!(s-x_{0})^{2j}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)(h_{n}-m-j-2)!\\
&\quad\quad\times\left(\binom{m+j}{2j}(h_{n}-m-j-1)-(2j+1)\binom{m+j+1}{2j+1}+\binom{m+j}{2j-1}(h_{n}-m-j-1)\right)\Bigg)\\
&=\frac{2^{2m+1}(s-\bar{x})}{(h-2m-3)!}\Bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{m-1}2^{2j+1}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!\binom{m+j+1}{2j}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times(s-x_{0})^{2j}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)\Bigg),\end{split}

where in the last equation we used the fact that

\binom{m+j}{2j}(h_{n}-m-j-1)-(2j+1)\binom{m+j+1}{2j+1}+\binom{m+j}{2j-1}(h_{n}-m-j-1)=\binom{m+j+1}{2j}(h_{n}-2m-2).

Summing up (
11.5
), (
11.6
) and (
11.7
), we obtain

(11.8)

A2=\frac{2^{2m+2}}{(h_{n}-2m-3)!}\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}2^{2j}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!\binom{m+j+1}{2j}(s-\bar{x})(s-x_{0})^{2j}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)\Bigg).

Now, we make the sum of (
11.4
), (
11.8
) and the last term in (
11.3
), we obtain

(11.9)

\displaystyle\overline{D}^{\beta+1}\left(S^{-1}_{L}(s,x)\right)=\frac{2^{2m+2}}{(h_{n}-2m-3)!}\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}2^{2j+1}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!\binom{m+j+1}{2j+1}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times(s-x_{0})^{2j+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{m}2^{2j}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!\binom{m+j+1}{2j}(s-\bar{x})(s-x_{0})^{2j}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)\Bigg)

\displaystyle+4^{2m+2}(2m+2)!(s-\bar{x})(s-x_{0})^{2m+2}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-3}(x).

Now we have to compute
\overline{D}^{\beta+2}(S^{-1}_{L}(s,x))
. By Lemma
11.2
and Proposition
4.13
, we get

(11.10)

\begin{split}&\overline{D}^{\beta+2}(S^{-1}_{L}(s,x))=\frac{2^{2m+2}}{(h_{n}-2m-3)!}\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}2^{2j+1}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!\binom{m+j+1}{2j+1}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\overline{D}\left((s-x_{0})^{2j+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)\right)\\
&+\sum_{j=0}^{m}2^{2j}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!\binom{m+j+1}{2j}\overline{D}\left((s-\bar{x})(s-x_{0})^{2j}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)\right)\Bigg)\\
&+4^{2m+2}(2m+2)!\overline{D}\left((s-\bar{x})(s-x_{0})^{2m+2}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-3}(x)\right)\\
&=\frac{2^{2m+2}}{(h_{n}-2m-3)!}\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}2^{2j+1}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!\binom{m+j+1}{2j+1}\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times(-2j-1)(s-x_{0})^{2j}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)\\
&+(s-\bar{x})\sum_{j=0}^{m}2^{2j+2}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!\binom{m+j+1}{2j+1}(m+j+2)(s-x_{0})^{2j+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-3}(x)\\
&+(s-\bar{x})\sum_{j=1}^{m}2^{2j}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!(-2j)\binom{m+j+1}{2j}(s-x_{0})^{2j-1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)\\
&+\sum_{j=0}^{m}2^{2j+1}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)\binom{m+j+1}{2j}(s-x_{0})^{2j}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)\\
&+(s-\bar{x})\sum_{j=0}^{m}2^{2j+2}(m+j+2)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!\binom{m+j+1}{2j}(s-x_{0})^{2j+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-3}(x)\Bigg)\\
&+4^{2m+2}(-2m-2)(2m+2)!(s-\bar{x})(s-x_{0})^{2m+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-3}(x)\\
&+4^{2m+2}2(h_{n}-2m-3)(2m+2)!(s-x_{0})^{2m+2}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-3}(x)\\
&+4^{2m+3}(2m+3)!(s-\bar{x})(s-x_{0})^{2m+3}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-4}(x).\end{split}

Now we split the computations in two parts. In the first part we make the summation of the terms without the factor
(s-\bar{x})
, and in the second part we make the summation of the terms with
(s-\bar{x})
.

First step B
: summation of all the terms without
(s-\bar{x})
. The terms we want to sum are

\displaystyle\frac{2^{2m+2}}{(h-2m-3)!}\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}2^{2j+1}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!

\displaystyle\times\binom{m+j+1}{2j+1}(-2j-1)(s-x_{0})^{2j}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{m}2^{2j+1}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)\binom{m+j+1}{2j}(s-x_{0})^{2j}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)

\displaystyle+2^{2m+2}2(h_{n}-2m-3)(h_{n}-2m-3)!(2m+2)!(s-x_{0})^{2m+2}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-3}(x)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{2m+2}}{(h_{n}-2m-3)!}\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}2^{2j+1}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!(s-x_{0})^{2j}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)

\displaystyle\times\left(-(2j+1)\binom{m+j+1}{2j+1}+(h_{n}-m-j-2)\binom{m+j+1}{2j}\right)\Bigg)

\displaystyle+2^{2m+2}2(h_{n}-2m-3)(h_{n}-2m-3)!(2m+2)!(s-x_{0})^{2m+2}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-3}(x)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{2m+3}}{(h_{n}-2m-4)!}\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m+1}2^{2j}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!

(11.11)

\displaystyle\times(s-x_{0})^{2j}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)\binom{m+j+1}{2j}\Bigg).

where we used the fact that:

\left(-(2j+1)\binom{m+j+1}{2j+1}+(h_{n}-m-j-2)\binom{m+j+1}{2j}\right)=(h_{n}-2m-3)\binom{m+j+1}{2j}.

Second step B
. Now we make the summation of the terms in (
11.10
) with the factor
(s-\bar{x})
. We note that in this summation we do not consider the last term since it appears in the right form without any needs to be manipulated. The terms we want to sum are

\begin{split}&\frac{2^{2m+2}(s-\bar{x})}{(h_{n}-2m-3)!}\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}2^{2j+2}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!\binom{m+j+1}{2j+1}(m+j+2)\\
&\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times(s-x_{0})^{2j+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-3}(x)\\
&+\sum_{j=1}^{m}2^{2j}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!(-2j)\binom{m+j+1}{2j}(s-x_{0})^{2j-1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)\\
&+\sum_{j=0}^{m}2^{2j+2}(m+j+2)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!\binom{m+j+1}{2j}(s-x_{0})^{2j+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-3}(x)\\
&+2^{2m+2}(-2m-2)(2m+2)!(h_{n}-2m-3)!(s-x_{0})^{2m+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-3}(x)\Bigg)\end{split}

In the second serie we make the change of index
j^{\prime}=j-1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{2^{2m+2}(s-\bar{x})}{(h_{n}-2m-3)!}\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}2^{2j+2}(m+j+2)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!\binom{m+j+1}{2j+1}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times(s-x_{0})^{2j+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-3}(x)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{m-1}2^{2j^{\prime}+2}(m+j^{\prime}+2)!(h_{n}-m-j^{\prime}-3)!(-2j^{\prime}-2)\binom{m+j^{\prime}+2}{2j^{\prime}+2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times(s-x_{0})^{2j^{\prime}+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-3}(x)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{m}2^{2j+2}(m+j+2)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!\binom{m+j+1}{2j}(s-x_{0})^{2j+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-3}(x)

\displaystyle+2^{2m+2}(-2m-2)(2m+2)!(h_{n}-2m-3)!(s-x_{0})^{2m+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-3}(x)\Bigg).

In formula
(B2)
, we make the summation of the term with
j=m
and the last term. So we have

\displaystyle\frac{2^{2m+2}(s-\bar{x})}{(h_{n}-2m-3)!}\Bigg(2^{2m+2}(2m+2)!(h_{n}-2m-2)!(s-x_{0})^{2m+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-3}(x)

\displaystyle+2^{2m+2}(2m+2)!(h_{n}-2m-2)!(2m+1)(s-x_{0})^{2m+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-3}(x)

\displaystyle+2^{2m+2}(-2m-2)(2m+2)!(h_{n}-2m-3)!(s-x_{0})^{2m+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-3}(x)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{2m+2}(s-\bar{x})}{(h_{n}-2m-3)!}\Bigg(2^{2m+2}(2m+2)!(h_{n}-2m-3)!

\displaystyle\times\left[(h_{n}-2m-2)+(2m+1)(h_{n}-2m-2)-2m-2\right](s-x_{0})^{2m+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-3}(x)\Bigg)

(11.12)

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{2m+2}(s-\bar{x})}{(h_{n}-2m-4)!}\Bigg(2^{2m+2}(2m+2)!(2m+2)(h_{n}-2m-3)!

\displaystyle\times(s-x_{0})^{2m+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-3}(x)\Bigg).

Now in
(B2)
we make the summation of the terms with
j=0,\dots,m-1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{2^{2m+2}(s-\bar{x})}{(h_{n}-2m-3)!}\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}2^{2j+2}(m+j+2)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!\binom{m+j+1}{2j+1}

(11.13)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times(s-x_{0})^{2j+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-3}(x)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j^{\prime}=0}^{m-1}2^{2j^{\prime}+2}(m+j^{\prime}+2)!(h_{n}-m-j^{\prime}-3)!(-2j^{\prime}-2)\binom{m+j^{\prime}+2}{2j^{\prime}+2}(s-x_{0})^{2j^{\prime}+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-3}(x)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}2^{2j+2}(m+j+2)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!\binom{m+j+1}{2j}(s-x_{0})^{2j+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-3}(x)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{2m+2}(s-\bar{x})}{(h_{n}-2m-3)!}\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}2^{2j+2}(m+j+2)!(h-m-j-3)!(s-x_{0})^{2j+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-3}(x)

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[(h-m-j-2)\binom{m+j+1}{2j+1}-(2j+2)\binom{m+j+2}{2j+2}+(h_{n}-m-j-2)\binom{m+j+1}{2j}\Bigg]\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{2m+2}(s-\bar{x})}{(h_{n}-2m-4)!}\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}2^{2j+2}(m+j+2)!(h_{n}-m-j-3)!\binom{m+j+2}{2j+1}

\displaystyle\times(s-x_{0})^{2j+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-3}(x)\Bigg),

where in the last equation we used the fact that

(h_{n}-m-j-2)\binom{m+j+1}{2j+1}-(2j+2)\binom{m+j+2}{2j+2}

+(h_{n}-m-j-2)\binom{m+j+1}{2j}=\binom{m+j+2}{2j+1}(h_{n}-2m-3).

Now we put together (
11.12
) and (
11.13
), we get

(11.14)

\begin{split}&B2=\frac{2^{2m+2}(s-\bar{x})}{(h_{n}-2m-4)!}\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m}2^{2j+2}(m+j+2)!(h_{n}-m-j-3)!\binom{m+j+2}{2j+1}\\
&\times(s-x_{0})^{2j+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-3}(x)\Bigg).\end{split}

Finally we sum (
11.11
), (
11.14
) and the last term in (
11.10
), we have

(11.15)

\begin{split}&\overline{D}^{\beta+2}\left(S^{-1}_{L}(s,x)\right)\\
&=\frac{2^{2m+3}}{(h_{n}-2m-4)!}\Bigg(\sum_{j=0}^{m+1}2^{2j}(m+j+1)!(h_{n}-m-j-2)!(s-x_{0})^{2j}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-2}(x)\binom{m+j+1}{2j}\\
&+\sum_{j=0}^{m}2^{2j+1}(m+j+2)!(h_{n}-m-j-3)!\binom{m+j+2}{2j+1}(s-\bar{x})(s-x_{0})^{2j+1}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-m-j-3}(x)\Bigg)\\
&+4^{2m+3}(2m+3)!(s-\bar{x})(s-x_{0})^{2m+3}\mathcal{Q}_{c,s}^{-2m-4}(x).\end{split}

This equation proves the inductive step and thus formula (
11.1
) is proved.
Finally formula (
11.2
) follows by applying the operator
\overline{D}
to formula (
11.3
). These computations have been already done to prove formula (
11.9
).
∎
